## Kind-hearted, Art-loving-
### Reader!

After I, a few years ago, translated various small treatises regarding the <term>Philosopher’s Stone</term> <note>original: "Stein der Weisen." The legendary substance capable of turning base metals into gold and granting long life.</note> from Latin into the High German language and saw them to print, and have now noticed that such a task is not unwelcome to the lovers of <term>Hermetic Philosophy</term> <note>original: "Hermetischen Philosophiæ." A philosophical and spiritual tradition based on writings attributed to Hermes Trismegistus, often associated with alchemy.</note> in Germany—especially to those who are not knowledgeable in the Latin language; I have, at the request of good friends, translated several more of the same into the High German language and collected them in this little book. To this volume, I have given the name <term>Alchemical Pleiades</term> <note>original: "Alchymistisch Sieben-Gestirn." Literally "Alchemical Seven-Star." The Pleiades is a star cluster known as the "Seven Sisters." The author uses this as a metaphor for a collection of seven foundational texts.</note> for the reason that the sailors in these lands guide themselves by the <term>Pleiades</term> <note>original: "Siebengestirn."</note> so that they may steer their course rightly—

<meta>catchword: steer—</meta>

<summary>In this preface, the translator explains his decision to publish a collection of seven alchemical treatises in German to assist those who cannot read Latin. He introduces the title "Alchemical Pleiades," using a nautical metaphor to suggest these texts will guide the reader through the "seas" of alchemical study.</summary>

<keywords>alchemical treatises, Hermetic Philosophy, Philosopher's Stone, translation, High German, navigation metaphor, Pleiades, Seven Stars</keywords>

lead / and may arrive in the desired haven: Likewise, I hope that this little book will be able to show a correct path to those who are eager for the highly noble art of <term>Alchemy</term> <note>original: Alchymiæ</note>, so that they may one day reach their desired goal. For although I am not a <term>practitioner</term> <note>original: Practicus; one who performs the physical laboratory work</note> of this art, nor a possessor of the High Work <note>The "Great Work" or creation of the Philosopher's Stone</note>, and cannot undertake it due to the frailty of my body and my age: nevertheless, in my opinion, I have come so far in the <term>theory</term> <note>original: Theoria</note> through God's grace and manifold reading, that I may well say for a truth: there is briefly contained in this small book exactly that which may be found described at great length in the six volumes of the <term>Chemical Theater</term> <note>original: Theatri Chymici; a massive 17th-century compendium of alchemical texts</note>, which are useful and necessary for the investigation of this excellent <term>secret</term> <note>original: Secreti</note>. ## The First Chapter.

Hermes / who is called a father of the Philosophers <note>Referring to Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary Hellenistic figure to whom many foundational alchemical and philosophical works were attributed.</note> / and lived approximately 2000 years before the birth of Christ / speaks thus: In so long a time of my life I have not ceased to seek and to explore / nor spared any labor / until I attained this Art and Science <note>original: "Kunst und Wissenschafft"; in this context, "Art" refers to Alchemy or the Great Work.</note> solely through the inspiration of the living God / who has deigned to reveal it to me, His servant. To rational men He has given the capacity to judge a thing and to express their opinion / but has allowed no one the opportunity to sin. However, if I did not fear the Day of Judgment / or the damnation of my soul / for the concealment of this science / I would have revealed nothing of it / nor indicated anything thereof to anyone. But I have wished to demonstrate my bounden duty to the faithful / just as the Author of the Faith <note>original: "Anheber des Glaubens"; referring to God or Christ as the source of belief.</note> deigned to bestow the same upon me.

So learn now / you children of wisdom, not in a fleshly manner / nor thoughtlessly, the science of the ancient Sages concerning the 4 Elements <note>The classical elements: Fire, Air, Water, and Earth, which formed the basis of alchemical theory.</note>...

<summary>This page introduces the legendary figure Hermes Trismegistus, who claims his alchemical knowledge was a divine gift that he is duty-bound to share with the faithful despite his initial desire for secrecy. It establishes the spiritual and moral gravity of the "Art" being discussed.</summary>
<keywords>Hermes Trismegistus, Alchemy, Divine Revelation, Four Elements, Philosophy, History of Science</keywords>

<meta>Continuity from previous page: The text continues the discussion on the elemental properties of the "Mercury" (Quecksilber) mentioned in the catchword.</meta>

(4)

stand; these <term>Elements</term><note>The four classical elements: Earth, Water, Air, and Fire</note> are passive according to their nature and their hidden operation. For their effect is very concealed, because none has its effect unless it is first put together: And nothing comes to perfection unless it has first completed its colors.

<margin>122</margin>
Therefore, you sons of the wise <note>A traditional address to students of alchemy</note> should know that the ancient <term>Philosophers</term><note>The historical alchemists and natural scientists</note> have divided their water, specifically into four parts: One to two, and three to one, of which the third part is the colors—namely, of the moisture that <term>coagulates</term><note>The process of thickening or turning a liquid into a solid</note> it. Two-thirds of the water, however, are the weights of the wise.

<margin>]</margin>
Take of the moisture one and a half ounces; and of the "Midday Redness"—that is, from the <term>Soul of Gold</term><note>The essential, fixed sulfur or "seed" of gold</note>—the fourth part, which is half an ounce; and of the yellow <term>Seyre</term><note>likely a corruption of 'Sirius' or a specific yellow pigment/tincture</note> likewise half an ounce; and half of <term>Auripigment</term><note>Orpiment, a yellow arsenic sulfide mineral</note>, which makes eight <insert>parts</insert>, that is, three ounces.

You should know that the <term>Vine of the Wise</term><note>A metaphor for the alchemical process that "grows" the Stone</note> is raised from three, and its wine is completed at the end with thirty. So understand now the operation: the boiling <note>original: "Kochung"</note> diminishes it, the <term>Tincture</term><note>The medicine or substance that transforms base metals into gold</note> increases it; for the Moon decreases after fifteen days, and in the third it increases. That is now the beginning and the end.

See, I have explained to you that which was so hidden; for the work is with you and by you, which you can grasp inwardly and have remaining in the earth or in the sea. So preserve now the...

--- Page 17 ---
<meta>Continuing from page 4, which likely introduced the foundational principles of the alchemical process. The catchword "See/" from the previous page leads into this description of Mercury.</meta>

Quick-silver <note>Mercury</note>, which is created in the innermost chambers, in which it is hardened. For it is the *Argentum vivum* <note>Latin: "living silver" or liquid mercury</note>, which takes its name from the earth left behind.

Therefore, whoever now hears my words should himself further investigate him who justifies no evildoer’s work and robs no benefactor of his reward. For I have discovered everything of this science that was hidden; the greatest secret and science I have revealed to those who have understanding.

Thus you shall now know, you seekers of this famous art and you children of wisdom, that the vulture who dwells upon the mountain cries out with a great voice: I am the white of the black, and the red of the white, and the yellow of the red, and certainly I speak the truth. And know that the beginning of the art is the raven, which flies without wings in the darkness of the night and the clarity of the day. From the bitterness that is in its throat, the color is taken; from its true body, the redness has come forth; and from its back, a pure water is taken.

This you shall now understand, and accept the gift of GOD, and hide it from all the unwise. In the caves of the metals it is hidden, which is the venerable stone, shining in color, an exalted spirit, and an open sea.

<summary>This page describes the symbolic stages of the alchemical Great Work, using the imagery of the vulture and the raven to represent the transformation of mercury into the Philosopher's Stone.</summary>
<keywords>mercury, argentum vivum, alchemy, vulture, raven, philosopher's stone, metals, nigredo</keywords>

--- Page 18 ---
Behold, now I have explained it to you; therefore, give thanks to God, who has taught you this science, for He loves those who are thankful. Accordingly, place it in a moist fire <note>original: "feuchtes Feuer" – likely a water bath or "balneum Mariae" used for gentle, steady heating</note> and let it boil, which increases the warmth of the moisture and consumes the dryness of the incombustibility, until the root appears; thereafter, extract from it the redness and the light part, until the third part remains behind.

You children of the wise, the philosophers <note>original: "Philosophi" – here referring specifically to alchemists</note> are called envious for this reason: not as if they begrudged it to the modest, the God-fearing, the righteous, and the wise, but rather to the ignorant and the wicked, who live without law and good works, so that they might not become powerful in evil to commit much sin, and the philosophers would thereafter have to give an account for it before God; for all the malicious are unworthy of wisdom.

You should know that I call this stone by its names; for they have called it the wife of Magnesia <note>original: "Weib der Magnesiæ" – a symbolic term for a primary alchemical substance</note>, or a hen, or white saliva, milk of the flying animals, and unburnt ash, so that they might hide it from the unwise who are without understanding, law, and good works; yet I have named it for the intelligent with a well-known name, which is the Philosopher's Stone <term>Philosopher's Stone: the legendary substance capable of turning base metals into gold and providing the elixir of life</term>.

So now preserve within it the water, the fire, and the volatile part of heaven, until the very last.

<summary>The author provides instructions for heating the alchemical substance in a moist fire and explains why alchemists use cryptic metaphors to hide their secrets from the morally unworthy. He lists several symbolic aliases for the Philosopher's Stone, such as the 'wife of Magnesia' and 'white saliva.'</summary>
<keywords>Philosopher's Stone, Magnesia, moist fire, alchemical secrecy, ethics of science, volatile spirit</keywords>

--- Page 19 ---
<meta>This page continues the introductory exhortations of the author, emphasizing the secrecy of the alchemical art and the moral requirements of the practitioner.</meta>

( 7 )

final end. However, I wish to have entreated all the **children of the wise** <note>A traditional alchemical term for initiated students or practitioners of the art.</note> by our Benefactor, who grants us the glory of His grace, that you will not reveal the name of this **Stone** <note>The Philosopher's Stone, the legendary substance capable of transmuting metals and healing the body.</note> to any fool, ignorant person, or unrefined individual. No one has ever given me anything that I have not repaid him for what he gave me, and I have not ceased to honor him and to show him kindness.

My son, this Stone is hidden; it is of many colors, and yet born in one color. Learn to recognize it and to keep it secret. If the Almighty permits it, you will thereby escape great illnesses, sadness, and everything that can harm and afflict; you will come from darkness to light, from the wilderness to a dwelling, and from anxiety and distress to joy and cheerfulness through His help.

## CHAPTER II.

My son, above all things I admonish you that you fear **GOD**, upon whom all your undertakings rest, as well as the union of every person who is set apart from the world. My son, everything that you hear, consider it with reason; for I certainly do not believe that you are deprived of reason or unwise. Therefore, receive my admonition well and consider it, and prepare your heart

<sig>A iv</sig>

joy.

## CHAPTER V.

My son, what is born of the <term>Raven</term> <note>The 'Raven' represents the 'Nigredo' or black stage, the initial phase of decomposition and purification in alchemy</note> is the beginning of this Art. Behold, with great length I have obscured the word for you and deprived it of light; that which is dissolved I have called the composite; that which is nearest I have called the furthest or most distant. Therefore, roast the things, and thereafter cook them with that which comes from the <term>body of the horse</term> <note>A common alchemical metaphor for horse manure, used to provide a gentle, steady heat for digestion</note> for seven, or 14, or 21 days.

Then it will become a <term>Dragon</term> <note>The dragon or serpent eating its own tail (Ouroboros) symbolizes the cyclical nature of the chemical process and the unification of opposites</note> that consumes its wings and kills itself. When that is accomplished, place it in a cloth rag and into the fire in the furnace, and guard it diligently so that it does not escape from the vessel.

<summary>This page describes the paradoxical nature of alchemical transformation, where destruction leads to improvement, and outlines the transition from the black 'Raven' stage to the symbolic 'Dragon' through steady heat.</summary>

And you should know that the time of the earth is in water, and it lasts so long until you lead it over it again. <note>The "earth" refers to the solid material or "body" in the alchemical vessel; "leading it over" likely refers to distillation or circulation.</note>

After it has now become soft and burnt, then take its brain <note>term: brain (original: Gehirn) — likely referring to the soft, white, or essential interior part of the matter</note> and rub it in the sharpest vinegar, or child's urine, until it becomes dark. When that has happened, it lives in putrefaction <note>term: putrefaction (original: Fäulung) — the stage of decomposition or "blackness" (nigredo) necessary for rebirth</note>; the dark clouds that were in it before it died in its body, those are changed. But if this is repeated as I have written, it dies once more, and as I have said, it lives thereafter.

In its life, however, as also in its death, we use the spirits <note>term: spirits (original: Spiritus) — the volatile substances or vapors that animate the fixed body</note>. For just as it dies when the spirits are taken away, so it becomes alive again when the spirits are given back to it, and it rejoices over them. But when you have come so far, you will find the same thing in truth as what you seek.

I must also tell you the signs of joy, and what makes its body fixed <note>term: fixed (original: fix mache) — the process of making a substance resistant to heat so it no longer evaporates</note>.

Through these figurative speeches your ancestors, after they reached their purpose, behold, there they died. Now I have shown you the goal and opened a book for the experienced; hidden things I have covered, the separated I have made so that it may be kept, different forms I have joined together, and joined the spirits to them. Accept this gift from GOD.

### CHAPTER VI.

You should rightly thank dear God, who bestows upon every wise man the Art <note>The "Art" refers to Alchemy, often called the Royal Art or Hermetic Art</note> which frees us from misery and poverty. With all its power and essence, and with demonstrable miraculous works, I will attempt it—humbly praying to the highest God that we may yet come to Him during our lifetime.

Therefore, you children of the wise: the ointments <note>original: "Unguenta"; in alchemy, these are often oily or fatty substances extracted during the process</note> which we extract from nails, hair, verdigris <note>original: "Spangrün"; a green pigment or crust formed on copper</note>, tragacanth <note>a natural gum</note>, and bones, are written of in the books of the ancients.

Furthermore, we must explain how one should prepare the ointment which contains the tincture <note>The "Tincture" is the potent medicinal or transformative essence sought by alchemists</note> within itself, which makes volatile substances hard and cleanses the sulfur <note>original: "Sulphur"</note>; this should be preferred above all other perfect ointments. For we know and recognize the preciousness of its vessel and essence, which is called the Divine Sulfur. It has a form like other ointments; it is the hidden and buried ointment which is of little outward appearance, and dwells within its body like fire dwells in trees and stones—which we must extract with very subtle art and understanding without burning it.

However, we must separate two sulfurs from others. You should know, my son, that he who does not know the difference knows the two sulfurs even less: not that there are exactly two...

<summary>This page introduces Chapter VI, focusing on the extraction of a "Divine Sulfur" or medicinal ointment from various organic and inorganic materials. The author emphasizes that this hidden essence must be extracted through subtle skill rather than harsh fire.</summary>
<keywords>Alchemy, Sulfur, Ointments, Tincture, Extraction, Divine Sulfur, Chemical Philosophy</keywords>

--- Page 33 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of chemical principles, specifically the preparation of the Tincture and the relationship between the elements.</meta>

( 21 )
B iij

These are the sulfurs <note>original: "Schweffel"; in alchemy, sulfur represents the active, combustible principle of a substance</note> which are sublimated from the ointment <note>original: "Unguent"; refers to a softened or oily state of the matter</note> of the stones to produce the tincture. However, when these two are mixed with the body, it becomes a perfect body. And it is to be known that both sulfurs provide the color <note>original: "tingiren"; to tinge or dye, referring to the alchemical transformation of base metals into gold</note> and yet flee from it; these must now be very carefully separated, and their flight must be prevented.

And you should know that heaven and earth—that is, the highest and the lowest—are joined together through a middle state. This middle state, however, is the form shared with heaven and with earth, which is the water. Among all things, water is the first which comes forth from this water: the second is the gold: the third is the dregs <note>original: "Hefen"; the sediment or lees</note> and the middling gold, which is nobler than the water and the dregs.

In these three, however, are the smoke, the blackness, and death. Therefore, we must drive away the smoke that stands above the water, the blackness from the ointment, and death from the dregs; and this is done through dissolution <note>term: dissolution; the process of dissolving a solid into a liquid, often the first step in the "Great Work"</note>. When that has happened, we possess the greatest wisdom and a secret of all secrets.

# THE OPENING
### of Raymond Lully
->concerning the<-
# PHILOSOPHERS' STONE.

The Sages testify that there is but one Stone, and that this same stone is composed solely of the four elements <note>The classical elements: Fire, Air, Water, and Earth</note>, and requires no external thing, unless it be of its own nature.

First of all, it is purified of all superfluity and phlegmatic, destructive matter <note>original: "phlegmatischen zerstörlichen Materi"</note>, which is its death, and which kills the spirit that has the power to make its earth alive. Therefore, it shall be purified, and the destructive phlegm separated from it by a subtle art, which I shall tell you.

For if it is not well purified, then its earth will neither be cleansed nor made white; likewise, no marriage <note>A term for the chemical union or 'conjunction' of substances</note> can occur between the body and the spirit.

And thus, that same spirit is then called the Spirit of the Stone <note>original: "des Lapidis Geist"</note>, which can make its dead body <note>original: "Corpus"</note> alive, from which composite body it departed through its preparation <note>original: "præparation"</note>.

And when, accordingly, the body [is raised] from death

Concerning such a resurrection, Hermes <note>Refers to Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary Hellenistic figure to whom many foundational alchemical texts are attributed.</note> speaks to all seekers of the Art and Truth as follows:
> O Son / take the soul of the stone and place it into our white foliated earth / which is volatile / unless you wish for it to lose its coarse elements. And know / that the soul holds it in such a form / that it cannot fly / when it ascends into the heavens / and descends upon the earth / for the Spirit <note>original: "Spiritus"</note> takes the power of the Body into itself in such a way / that there is nothing / that can prevail against it. Therefore, first kill the Body through destruction / before you do anything else / and afterwards gradually bring it back to life / until it reaches such a middle state of corporeality and spirituality / which we call the Medium / which has communion with the nature of the two extremes / namely with the soul and the body.

<meta>This page continues the philosophical discussion of the tripartite nature of man or matter, specifically focusing on the "Spirit" as the mediating force between Body and Soul.</meta>

And in such a manner we say that the Spirit is the life of the Soul when it encompasses the Soul within itself. Thus, we also call the Spirit a body because of its tangible and more visible corporeality, of which it is composed and by which it is also placed in a specific location. And this is the reason why this <term>Spiritus</term><note>Latin: Spiritus; here referring to the "breath of life" or a subtle vaporous substance that connects the physical to the divine.</note> contains within itself the nature of the body.

And beyond these two aforementioned properties, you should know this third thing between the body and the soul, which we call the "Middle" <note>The "Medium" or bridge.</note>, which can unite and bind them together without separation. And thereafter, the Spirit encompasses the Soul within itself, through which the deceased <term>Corpus</term><note>Latin: Corpus; the physical body or material substance.</note> is made living again.

So now, this is the moving cause: that the <term>conjunctio</term><note>Latin: conjunctio; a "conjunction" or sacred marriage of elements in alchemy.</note> of the body and the spirit occurs through natural love and a friendly <term>disposition</term><note>Latin: dispositio; an arrangement or state of being.</note>.

For such a friendly disposition (arrangement) and conjunction of love is that which brings about the union of the spirit and the body, so that they become one substance of an inseparable body, in which is the thickness of the body and the power, with all those figures which primarily enter into the essence from the subtle spirit.

Its thickness is also far displaced into the substance with subtle spirit, through such a subtle manner, in such a way that there is no bodily eye—

Following this, my son, you can understand that the secret of the work of the <term>Philosophers</term><note>original: "Philosophorum"; referring to the alchemists of old</note> is divided into two parts. The first teaches how to prepare the <term>Sulphur</term><note>In alchemy, Sulphur represents the active, combustible, and masculine principle of matter, not merely the chemical element.</note>; the second teaches how to transform that Sulphur into a fixed and flowing <term>Medicine</term><note>The "Medicine" or Tincture is the final substance capable of transmuting metals or healing the body.</note>.

As <term>Morienes</term><note>A legendary 7th-century alchemist, said to be the teacher of King Khalid ibn Yazid.</note> teaches in his book, where he says: "Our entire masterpiece must necessarily occur in two creations, and these are alternating causes in nature that are very closely related to one another." For when the first creation has been fulfilled, the second begins for the work of the Medicine. And when the second is completed, the entire masterpiece has reached its final conclusion.

Now, son of truth, understand that in the first operation of our art, we purify and prepare the materials for the creation of their Sulphur. And when that same Sulphur is prepared, we then compose and create in the second labor only the Medicine, which will have such great power and capability as shall be seen.

And for that reason, you must first bring their Sulphur to pass, for without it, no one can make a perfect <term>Elixir</term><note>The "Elixir" is the refined substance sought by alchemists to achieve transmutation.</note>. And when you have made the Sulphur, then the truly Philosophical Work begins.

You must also not feel any vexation in nourishing it, and do not hurry; for it is written: He who has no patience, and does not [read] in our books...

## Concerning the Fixation and Composition of the Tincture.

Now I will speak of the fixation <term>fixation: the process of making a volatile substance stable under heat</term> of the Air <note>In alchemy, "Air" often refers to a volatile spirit or gas released during heating</note> which carries the Tincture within itself. And precisely this same nature, which is a cause of uniformity, separates itself from all that is non-uniform when it can be transformed into all elemental qualities; and therefore they can change themselves and transform the nature from one quality into another—now into dryness, then into moisture, and so with others—according as the division of nature is strengthened through the joining together of its elements.

And Son, understand this: that our Stone <note>The Philosopher's Stone, the goal of the alchemical Great Work</note> does not come to be otherwise than from a pure nature alone, which every wise man will know through two paths: first, through contemplative wisdom—that is, through the moderation of the intellect—and through experience, with perfect enlightenment.

<meta>The catchword "Das" from the previous page leads into the first chapter heading on this page. This page serves as the preface to the "Elucidatio Testamenti," a summary work intended to clarify the author's previous, more cryptic writings.</meta>

->(59)<-

Although we have written very many books concerning various works of our **Philosophical Art** <note>The "Philosophical Art" refers to Alchemy, specifically the pursuit of the Philosopher's Stone.</note>; nevertheless, we wish to prefer this, our latest little book, over the others, which is rightly entitled by us *Elucidatio Testamenti*, an **Explanation** <note>original: "Elucidatio Testamenti"</note>. For whereas we have hidden our meaning with great long-windedness in the philosophical writings of our *Testament* and *Codicil* <note>original: "Codicilli"; these refer to two of the most famous works attributed to the alchemist Raymond Lully</note>, we show that same meaning quite clearly in this our little book, and explain it in the shortest possible way, so that it will no longer be necessary to write any more books. For because the *Composition* <note>original: "Compositio"; the process of putting together or synthesizing the alchemical work</note> or preparation of our books is nothing other than an artistic dexterity of a subtle *Genius* <note>original: "Ingenij"; referring to the natural talent or wit used to craft riddles</note> to hide our art, which is shown at great length in our books; so now shall the same be brought here out of the darkness into a bright light, which before me no *Philosopher* <note>original: "Philosophus"; in this context, an alchemist</note> has ever dared to do. And this little book we compose in six chapters, in which the entire secret of this art is revealed with very clear words. Among the six chapters:

The First is concerning the *Matter* <note>original: "Materia"</note> of our Stone.
The Second is concerning the Vessel.
The Third is concerning the Furnace.

<summary>In this preface, the author introduces the "Elucidatio Testamenti" as a definitive and clear summary of his alchemical secrets, intended to simplify the complex allegories found in his earlier works. He outlines the first three chapters, focusing on the raw materials, the equipment, and the furnace required for the Great Work.</summary>

<keywords>Alchemy, Elucidatio Testamenti, Philosopher's Stone, Materia, Alchemical Furnace, Raymond Lully, Hermetic Philosophy</keywords>

# Chapter I.
## Concerning the Material of our Stone.

**N**ow we shall first begin to provide instruction regarding the Material <note>original: "Materia"</note> of our Stone. For we have introduced foreign things into our Mastery <note>original: "Magisterio"</note> for the sake of comparison, even though there is only one single thing belonging to our Stone, to which we add nothing, nor do we take anything away. We have also described three Stones, namely a Mineral, an Animal, and a Vegetable stone, yet there is only one single Stone in our Art.

You dear sons (of Wisdom), we wish to give you to understand that the Stone is composed of three: namely of Body, Soul, and Spirit. It is called Mineral because it is a mineral <note>original: "Minera," defined here as an earth-juice or a thing dug out of the earth</note>. It is called Animal because it possesses a soul. It is called Vegetable because it grows and is multiplied <note>increased</note> like the things that grow from the earth. In this Stone lies hidden every secret of our masterpiece, which is Gold, Silver, and the Water of Life <note>original: "Aqua vitæ"</note>. The Water of Life, however, is the Soul.

<meta>catchword: and</meta>

<summary>This page introduces the fundamental materials of the Alchemical Stone, explaining that while it is described as mineral, animal, and vegetable, it is ultimately a single substance composed of body, soul, and spirit.</summary>
<keywords>Alchemy, Philosopher's Stone, Materia, Body Soul and Spirit, Mineral Animal and Vegetable, Aqua Vitae, Decoction, Multiplication</keywords>

and the life of the bodies, through which our stone is made living and propagated. And for this reason, we call it a heaven, and a *quintessence* <note>original: "quintam essentiam"; the "fifth element" or purest essence in alchemy</note>, also an unburnable oil, and by countless other names. For it is almost imperishable and incorruptible, like the heavens in the unceasing circulation of their movement.

Thus you have, in clear revelation and without any circumlocution, the material of our *Stone* <note>original: "Lapidis"; the Philosopher's Stone</note>.

## Chapter II.
### Concerning the Vessel.

Now we wish to speak of our vessel. Here, my son, open your ears so that you may understand our meaning. Although we have described many kinds of vessels with hidden words in our books, our meaning is directed toward no other vessel than a single one, which we wish to openly indicate and make understood here, in which our work is performed from the beginning to the end of the entire masterpiece. Thus, our vessel is a composite work, for there are two vessels joined together with their *alembics* <note>original: "alembicis"; the cap or lid of a still used for distilling</note>, of equal size and height, where the beak of one goes into the belly of the other, so that the same, through the effect of the

# Chapter III.
## Concerning the Furnace.

We wish now to say something regarding our furnace: however, it will be quite difficult to recount the secret of our furnace, which the ancient philosophers <note>original: "Philosophi"</note> kept so very hidden.
We have indeed mentioned many kinds of furnaces in our books, yet we use only one single furnace, which is called the Athanor <term>Athanor: from the Arabic 'al-tannur', a self-feeding furnace designed to maintain a constant temperature for long periods</term>, which is translated as an "immortal fire"; for it provides a fire that lasts constantly at the same degree from the beginning until the end of our Stone <note>original: "Lapidis", referring to the Philosopher's Stone</note>, and which makes the same living and mature.
My son, pay attention to our words, and you will understand that our furnace is composed of two pieces or parts, where the joints meet correctly and are well sealed. Its nature is as follows:
Make a furnace large or small, according to whether there is much or little matter <note>original: "Materi"</note>. For the quantity of the matter requires a large furnace; if there is

For the nature of fire requires such a furnace alone, and no other.

The sealing of the joints on such a furnace of ours is called the <term>Sigillum Hermetis<note>original: "Sigillum Hermetis"; the Seal of Hermes, referring to an airtight or "hermetic" seal</note></term> and the seal of the wise, because it is known only to the wise; it has never been explicitly described by any <term>Philosopher<note>original: "Philosopho"; here referring to an alchemist or seeker of natural wisdom</note></term>, but is kept in the wisdom of God and preserved in His almighty power.

## Chapter IV.
### Concerning the Fire.

Although we have taught in our books of a threefold fire—namely, a natural fire, an unnatural fire, and a fire against nature—as well as other diverse ways of using our fire; nevertheless, we wish here to make known only a single fire, which is composed of many things. It is the greatest secret to come into the knowledge of this fire, for it is not a human but an angelic task to reveal this heavenly gift.

However, so that the curse which the Philosophers

left to their descendants, may not fall upon us; so we pray to GOD that this treasure of our secret fire may come into the hands of no one else but the wise.

My son, incline your ears to hear that our fire is composed of two things. For the Creator of all things has created two things which, among others, are suitable for this fire: namely, horse manure and unslaked lime. The mixing of these creates our fire, the artistic invention of which is conducted as follows.

Take one part <term>Ventrem equi</term> <note>Latin: "belly of the horse," a common alchemical term for horse manure used as a gentle, steady heat source</note>, that is, horse manure which is well digested, and one part pure, living lime <note>also known as quicklime or unslaked lime</note>. These mixed together and packed tightly upon one another in our furnace, with the vessel containing the <term>Materia Lapidis</term> <note>Latin: "matter of the Stone," the base substance being transformed into the Philosopher's Stone</note> placed in the middle, and the furnace diligently sealed—then you will have an excellent fire without light and without coals. When it is thus placed in its furnace, it cannot be or act otherwise than as necessity requires.

# Chapter VI.
## Concerning the Tincture and Multiplication / or the Increase of our Stone.

Finally, we wish to speak of the <term>Tincture</term> <note>The power of the Stone to transform base metals into gold</note>
and <term>Multiplication</term> <note>The process of increasing the quantity and potency of the alchemical medicine</note>, which are then the end
and the conclusion of the entire <term>Mastery</term> <note>original: "Magisterij"; the completed alchemical work</note>.

<summary>This page explains that all alchemical operations are unified within a single "decoction" or cooking process lasting ten months. The author notes that complex terminology is used intentionally to hide the secret of the "Masterpiece" from the unworthy.</summary>
<keywords>decoction, Great Work, secrecy, ten months, Tincture, Multiplication, Magisterium, alchemy</keywords>

--- Page 78 ---
<meta>The previous page likely discussed the initial preparation of the Stone; this page transitions into the specific methods of increasing its power and quantity through repetition.</meta>

(66)

We have indeed indicated various *modes of projection* <note>original: "modos projectionis"; the method of casting the alchemical catalyst onto a base metal to transform it</note> of our Tincture <note>The "Tincture" is the refined essence or "Philosopher's Stone" in liquid or powder form</note> in our books; yet there is no difference between our Tincture and Multiplication, and neither can occur without the other. For our Stone does not tinge <note>transform or color</note> unless it is first tinged itself; but when it is tinged, its quantity is multiplied: And when our Stone is multiplied, whether it be white or red, it is also tinged.

Dear son, drive away from yourself the darkening of your understanding, so that you may be able to understand the *secret of secrets* <note>original: "arcanum arcanorum"</note> that we have hidden with clever diligence in our books, which here comes out from the deeply hidden into the manifest light.

Hear and understand that our Multiplication is nothing other than a repetition of our initially composed Work. For in the first repetition, one part of our Stone tinges 3 parts of an imperfect body <note>base metals like lead or tin</note>; and by so many parts it increases, and is multiplied in quantity.

In the second repetition, 1 part tinges 7 parts. In the third, 1 part tinges 15 parts.

In the fourth, 1 part tinges 31 parts. In the fifth, 63 parts. In the sixth, 127; and thus it is always multiplied and increases by so many parts even without end. If it were repeated 24 times, then 1 part would tinge

## A Brief Preface.

Next to the invocation of the name of
Christ, I have considered that in the
works of Nature—which are to be accom-
plished with the help of Art—most artists
understand the books of the philosophers <note>The "philosophers" here refers to the ancient alchemists.</note>
only according to the outer shells of the words.
Thus, it is impossible for them to work correctly,
and they fall thereby into great damage of their
possessions and goods.

Therefore, I wish to set forth a model in this
treatise and partly explain the Art, just as I
found it in a book which was an explanation
of Aristotle’s <term>Meteorologica</term> <note>original: "derer Dinge / so in der Lufft entstehen" — referring to Aristotle's works on atmospheric phenomena, often interpreted alchemically in the Middle Ages.</note>.
Aristotle sent this book in quiet peace to Alexander <note>Alexander the Great, traditionally depicted as Aristotle's pupil.</note>,
and it begins thus:
> Take the animal and vegetable
> stone, etc. <note>The "stone" refers to the Elixir or Philosopher's Stone; "animal and vegetable" indicates the source materials used in this specific process.</note>
Pope Honorius had this book translated from
Hebrew into Latin by a Greek who knew the
Art perfectly.

For this reason, he passes over the various
works of all metals and spirits <note>original: "Spirituum" — volatile substances like mercury or sulfur.</note>,
because Nature brings her degrees <note>original: "Gradus" — the stages of refinement or heat.</note>
to the final, perfect end. He indicates what
should be done if one were led deeply into
error and deceived by seemingly clever
subtleties regarding the metals.

--- Page 84 ---
Listen, my son; hear the teaching of the father of philosophers, Aristotle the Spagyrist <note>Aristoteles the Spagyri: A pseudo-Aristotelian reference common in alchemy, where "Spagyrist" refers to the art of separating and recombining elements</note>, who commands that you take the animal, vegetable, and mineral stone, etc. From this it is manifest that the stone is animal, which, like a serpent, is released, begotten, and divided from the corruption of perfect human nature with diligence between two mountains; it falls down and is locked in the pit of its cave, governed, established, and preserved with moderate heat.

This dragon is of a warm nature and seeks an exit before birth; it wishes to spoil its fruit and desires a miscarriage. Through all too violent heat or cold it is spoiled; primarily in too great a heat it must suffocate, and that which is sought can nevermore become of it. The poison of the dragon, however, goes through the veins, which in the final vessel is entirely taken away from him. And just as the subtle nature in said veins was the cause for his growth and life, and brought him to a perfect nature, so also shall this be your intended goal and the first step toward perfection.

In every natural birth of a perfect nature, a convenient time of 40 days is given. Because now, from the joining and revolution of the 7 planets through the circle of the signs <note>The Zodiac</note>, through the change—

...of the four elements <term>elements</term><note>The four classical elements: Earth, Water, Air, and Fire</note> is correctly changed and recognized. Everything that is contained beneath the circle of the moon <note>The "sublunary" sphere, referring to the earthly realm where things are subject to change and decay</note> is likewise treated in this work through boiling out, evaporation, ascending, descending, and the extraction of the aforementioned four elements; finally, these four are converted into one.

Just as a meadow is adorned with flowers by the dew of heaven, which receive their colors, scent, and various properties from that very same earth—the cause of which is the natural governance of the super-celestial bodies, which receive their form, fruit, and effect in the earth—and this occurs because of the blackness of the earth, the whiteness of the water, the greenness of the air, the redness of the fire, and the mingling of nature.

The cause is that in the true conception of our dragon <term>dragon</term><note>A common alchemical symbol for primordial matter or the volatile substance being refined</note>, all perfect as well as mixed colors will appear. And this dragon begins to look miraculous; it becomes thick like a very black toad, and because of its lowliness and total dissolution, it desires to be released from its sadness. It raises and spreads its white wings and begs that they may be cut off from it entirely and without great effort. This can happen when, by the same governance of heat, its animated sweat <note>original: "beseelter Schweiß"; refers to the condensed vapors or "spirit" released during heating</note>—which is air and water—and the water alone is extracted moderately and slowly. The Work often consists not in the quantity, but in the variation of time. For in the first three changes, it shall be dried. But on the twentieth day, the Earth is still powerful; therefore, in the first three changes, the Earth shall be moistened with its Water up to the third part, until it is nourished to its perfect state—which is not only dry, but also no longer volatile in any way.

For the dryness of the Earth causes the spirits to be suffocated and their properties to be destroyed. And this is the first and perfect conjunction: For this, after a balance is struck, will have two parts of the Earth and always a third part of the Water. For the Fire is hidden under the Water, which is white and clear, and the Earth under the Air, whose greenness is transformed into a white color.

We should seek no other <term>Elements<note>original: "Elementa"</note></term> than Earth and Water: For Air and Fire are enclosed within those boundaries and effects.

Find grace with God, nor favor among men.

One needs nothing more than one thing, one vessel: for it is a single <term>Medicine</term><note>In alchemy, the "Medicine" refers to the Elixir or Philosopher's Stone capable of perfecting base metals and the human body.</note> and a single process—not indeed of the hands, but the operation of Nature and its bringing together into one.

However, every heat or cold must be according to its own nature and must not exceed it, unless you intend to labor in vain. Natures embrace one another within their own boundaries; they conceive and complete their work where there is no excessive heat or cold; they give birth and bring forth fruit. Therefore, preserve the moderate heat from the cold and from every foreign thing.

The first work is accomplished through natural <term>solution</term><note>dissolving a solid into liquid</note>, <term>distillation</term>, <term>conjunction</term><note>the joining of opposites, often symbolized as a chemical marriage</note>, and <term>calcination</term><note>reduction to a powder through heat</note>, as well as the composition of its water, in a round, spherical vessel that is sealed everywhere and made of suitable material.

Then you have correctly hit upon the work, provided the rules set above are sufficient for the worker. But whatever is less or more than this is of evil, and that which is sought cannot come from it.

The second work of our <term>Dragon</term><note>The "Dragon" is a common alchemical symbol for the volatile matter or the raw "First Matter" that must be tamed and transformed.</note>, however, is that he be moistened after he has reached the degree of whiteness, with two-thirds of his sweat and milk; for it is necessary that the earth becomes water, and if it is properly ruled—

If the dragon allows itself to be easily dissolved, then you can make the **proportion** <note>original: "proportion" - the ratio of liquid to solid</note> of its water double in relation to it; and if it should be too much, then **cure** <note>original: "curire" - to treat or correct</note> its weakness, as was mentioned above.

But where the color of the dragon changes—which is the second error in this work—let it stand longer with its water without **rectification** <note>original: "rectificirung" - the process of refining or purifying a substance</note> of its earth, until the earth begins to appear like fish-eyes. And then moisten it, as before, and do not stop until it becomes entirely a **crystalline** water, clear, without impurity; and from now on, at the end, it shall no longer be fed nor stopped up, but shall be preserved in its vessel from uncleanness.

The color of the heavenly water, however, will be like the color of the firmament <note>The sky or the heavens; likely indicating a bright blue or clear hue</note>. Its scent will be like the scent of a precious ointment of spices. But guard yourself, that you do not drink of it, because of its penetrating and burning power, except only after its **projection** <note>original: "projection" - the final stage of alchemy where the medicine is applied to transform a substance</note>, when it is applied to others, as I will mention at the end.

But you should take it with the hundredth part of common water or milk, after the stomach is well filled, for the reason that the internal nature, as it were, opens itself up and dissolves. Through the power of this **medicine**, nature can be preserved for one hundred and fifty years.

<meta>Continuity from page 81: The text continues discussing the philosophical and medicinal properties of the alchemical water, linking it to the wisdom of the ancients.</meta>

Which practice of *Aristotle*—as *Arnold the Greek* <note>Likely referring to Arnaldus de Villa Nova, a famous 13th-century physician and alchemist</note> says in the preface to the *Book of the Apple* <note>original: "Buch vom Apffel"; a pseudo-Aristotelian work titled 'Liber de Pomo' concerning the soul and immortality</note>, and according to a translator of the Holy Scripture—possesses a power and virtue equal to the water in Paradise. By its very nature, this water makes a person immortal, healthy, fresh, joyful, and everlasting.

From this water, all true philosophers have derived their lives and incalculable wealth. Therefore, it is better to study wisdom than to become rich, for the former encompasses within itself wealth and all physical and earthly happiness.

For this reason, this ensouled stone is called *Rebis* <note>A central alchemical concept meaning "two-fold thing," representing the union of opposites like male and female or spirit and matter</note>, because it contains within itself the perfection of all things. It is also called an "Egg" because of its roundness, being spherical, or because it possesses the form and shape of the Great World <note>The Macrocosm or the Universe</note>, which has a round shape and encloses and contains all other things within itself.

See how openly I have shown you the truth of the Scripture. Yet you should know that this work is divided into five parts: namely, the conception of the matter, which must be of a healthy, right, and well-disposed nature, of moderate stature, and of the male gender; fresh, flowing, red, evaporated, and it should always be warm, and in its heavenly course it shall, in time, become powerful, dear, and pleasant.

It is to be held as a general rule, however, that in this work, never more than two **elements** are seen: namely, **Earth** and **Water**. For within the Earth, the **Fire** is contained, and within the Water, the **Air**. Therefore, **Aristotle** says in the *Secrets to Alexander* <note>original: "Secretis Secretorum," a popular medieval text attributed to Aristotle</note>: "Make the earth perfect with its heavenly water, and your whole work will become perfect."

This same Earth is an earth of the **fifth essence** <term>Quinta Essentia: the purest, most potent extract of a substance, beyond the four physical elements</term>; for it is brought to such a degree, insofar as the fifth essence is the highest effect of nature and power in every thing. For by its own nature, it cannot be brought to another external degree. And in such a degree is all the power and property that one can possibly have in a body under the sun; for its nature ceases to stand in the fourth degree, but it takes on the fifth from the outside, from the elements.

Note now this one great secret:
that every **Quintessence** <note>Latin: *Quinta Essentia*; the "fifth essence" or purest medicinal core of a substance</note> is in itself a
preserver of the health of every body.
And it possesses within itself all the power and effect
of every thing that is of that very same com-
plexion and essence, and of no other.

For instance, the **Quintessence of Poison** works
to bring health only to those of a poisonous nature, and likewise
with others. Thus, the **Quintessence of Me-
tals** cures all weaknesses of metals.
From this, one can now conclude that the science
of the **Philosopher's Stone** is true,
unfailing, and without falsehood.

Secondly, it is to be noted that if one seeks
the science of curing the infirmi-
ties of metals, and does not do so through
their **fifth essence**, he labors in vain; his
search is for nothing, and he finds neither end,
nor salvation, nor health.

Therefore, when the **Earth** is artificially brought
to its fifth essence, and the **Water** in like
manner, then from the two united together
arises **Perfection**. Through this, all the Phi-
losophers <note>Referring here to alchemists, often called "natural philosophers"</note> have possessed that which was neces-
sary for them for the body's health and the
sustenance of life.

One must also take care that the evaporation of the *matter* does not last longer than its heat remains, which sometimes tends to last for about 4 hours. From this we can learn that in this work one must do nothing else but let the vessel with the *matter* stand in a separate place covered with a cloth, so that no dust falls into it, and the accidental moisture of the cloth is entirely driven out from the vessel. And this must be done for two reasons: first, because of the rapid transformation of nature; and second, for the expulsion of accidental moistures, which might cause the living *matter* in our stone to be destroyed, preventing it from reaching the ash-gray color.

And the perfect dissolution in this work is its most complete *fifth essence* <note>original: "Quinta essentia"; the purest, most concentrated medicinal or spiritual force extracted from a substance.</note> / which contains within itself all medicinal power / and a wonderfully beautiful scent / full of all sweetness.

There are three signs of a complete dissolution. First / that it is truly water in itself without impurity: Next / that it appears white as milk on the surface: Third / that it is sweeter than honey to the taste upon the tongue. *Note.* This aforementioned *matter* <note>original: "Materia"</note> however, becomes hard of its own accord with warm and dry fire / and dissolves itself in warm and moist fire. And this water is constant and everlasting / which, because of its miraculous effect, contains the power and virtue of all stones within itself: Therefore, it is also called by the names of all stones / such as <term>mercury</term><note>original: "Quecksilber"</note> / <term>sulfur</term><note>original: "Schweffel"</note> / <term>orpiment</term><note>original: "Operment"; a deep-colored, arsenic-rich mineral used as a pigment and in alchemy.</note> / and it is called by many other names—

<summary>This page details the rectification of "earth" and "water," describing the sensory signs of a successful alchemical dissolution and the universal nature of the resulting "fifth essence."</summary>
<keywords>rectification, dissolution, fifth essence, alchemy, mercury, sulfur, orpiment, medicinal power</keywords>

it is called; and yet it is none of these in its physical nature, but rather in its power and virtue. For it is a <term>Mercury<note>original: "Quecksilber." In alchemy, Mercury represents the fluid, transformative principle of matter.</note></term> because it possesses within itself a perfect power and life. It is also the incombustible <term>Sulphur<note>original: "Schweffel." Sulphur represents the active, combustible, and "soul" principle of the substance.</note></term>, and therefore it carries all signs with it according to its primary meaning: For a sign does not take its name from its final meaning, but from its most noble and principal one.

The sixth and final error consists in the constant maintenance of the fire and the application of the <term>Tincture<note>The "Tincture" is the concentrated medicinal or transformative essence sought by alchemists.</note></term>. For if an interruption should occur in the heat, or a breach in the vessel or in the timing, the incineration of the work would follow. For the time is uniform and of one kind: namely, in the <term>Putrefaction<note>original: "Putrefactio." The stage of "rotting" or breaking down matter to its base state, usually associated with the color black.</note></term> 40 days for the Whiteness, and for the Redness 40 days. For the perfect dissolution 40 days, provided that the governance of the fire has been employed in the proper manner.

In the first period it is called a "stone and yet no stone"; in the second, a "stone"; in the third, <term>Living Silver<note>original: "Argentum vivum." Another term for Mercury or Quicksilver.</note></term>; in the fourth, a "Ruby." These names are given to it by <term>Hermes<note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary Hellenistic figure and purported author of the Hermetic Corpus.</note></term>, and in part by <term>Aristotle</term> and <term>Morienus<note>A 7th-century legendary alchemist known for his dialogues with King Khalid.</note></term>.

The sign in the putrefaction is the thinness accompanied by the deepest blackness. The sign of the distillation or <term>Calcination<note>original: "Calcinirung." The process of heating a substance to high temperatures to drive off volatile substances or achieve oxidation.</note></term> of the stone is dryness with an ash-color. The sign of the Whiteness is that it appears as white

The sign of the dissolution is that nothing remains undissolved, but rather that it becomes entirely like a standing, clear water, without impurity. The application <note>original: "Auftragung"; the process of projection or adding the catalyst to the base metal</note> of our Stone, however, is such: first, one part of the mentioned blessed Stone, which has been hardened in warm ashes, shall be applied to 10 parts of common mercury <note>original: "Mercurij"</note> that has been made warm, so that the thought-of mercury is thus incorporated with the Stone <note>original: "Lapide"</note> and made fixed <note>term: "fix" (fixed); a substance that can endure fire without evaporating</note>. Then it shall be cast upon 100 parts. And if it is cast on a second time, it has the power to tinge <note>term: "tingiren" (tinge); to transform the color and nature of a metal, usually into gold or silver</note> a thousand parts; and for the third time, a thousand times a thousand parts. Indeed, let it be cast upon whatever metals one wishes, be they ever so imperfect, it will nonetheless tinge them according to the property of the medicine, namely white or red <note>note: white for silver, red for gold</note>, so that no mineral or metal can be found more perfect, unless it be for curing the weaknesses of the body. For the gold from the ore mines has essentially something divine within it in its first creation and in its first work.

There is, however, no difference between the White and the Red <note>The "White" and "Red" refer to the *albedo* and *rubedo*, the final two major stages of the Great Work in alchemy.</note> except for a longer decoction <term>decoction: the process of heating or boiling a substance to extract its essence</term> without water after the perfect whiteness of our Stone has been achieved, until the red color appears more perfectly and the earth has been entirely turned to ashes; and then the work is to be begun just as was said at the beginning regarding the White.

This teaching, though described with few words and sayings, can be sufficient for one who understands it rightly and thoroughly, since we know that for those who love **GOD**, all things must work together for the best.

# The Beginning of the Rose Garden
## In which the most secret Mystery of the Sages is contained
## Described by John Dastin, an Englishman and well-experienced Philosopher. <note>John Dastin (c. 1293–1386) was a famous English alchemist known for his correspondence with Pope John XXII regarding the transmutation of metals.</note>

<image-desc>Decorative floral band separates the page number from the title. A second, different decorative band of repeating scroll-like patterns sits below the first.</image-desc>

First, it is to be known that this pleasant and very precious work has been assembled from the books of the Ancients. We wish, however, to briefly summarize everything here, so that to you, dear reader, the most excellent as well as the most certain mystery may be sufficiently revealed. We call this short compendium a <term>Rose Garden</term> <note>original: "Rosarium," a common title for medieval alchemical treatises, implying a collection of "flowers" of wisdom.</note> for this reason: because we have plucked and gathered it for your benefit from the books of the Ancients, like roses from the thorn-hedges. And because everything that runs contrary to truth or good reason is harmful to the truth, we intend to teach briefly—using sayings that agree entirely with the truth, in clear speech and proper order, word for word, with sufficient reasons attached—everything we have found in their books that serves for the completion of the work.

## CHAPTER I.

All works / which the benevolent GOD has created / move as if in a circle and are perfect / they return again to the place from whence they came / driven around in a roundness. For in the beginning of creation when GOD the Lord established Nature as the beginning of all things / He made four simple bodies <note>The four classical elements: Earth, Water, Air, and Fire.</note> / from which He afterwards composed all mixed bodies.

But of the mixed bodies He made some / which have reason / some sensitivity / or senses / others which grow out of the earth / and others which originate within the earth.

And indeed all those who have reason / HE created from the most subtle part of the elements in His own image. Therefore also our heart is not restless / until it comes to Him again <note>A reference to St. Augustine’s famous dictum: "Our heart is restless until it rests in Thee."</note>. For the subtle part of all elements ascends up to the fire / which is among the stars. Since we are now created from such a part, so—

(104)
<meta>The catchword "Es" from the previous page likely introduces the continuation of the argument regarding the reduction of metals to their primary matter.</meta>

Matter must be brought back. Therefore the <term>Philosopher<note>original: "Philosophus"; likely referring to Aristotle, who was frequently cited as the ultimate authority in alchemical texts.</note></term> says: The masters of <term>Alchemy<note>original: "Alchimiæ"</note></term> should know that the <term>Species<note>original: "Species"; here referring to the specific types or categories of metals.</note></term> of metals cannot be truly and properly transformed unless they are first brought back into their original <term>Matter<note>original: "Materiam"; the "prima materia" or formless base substance of all things.</note></term>; and then they are transformed and turned into something different than they were before. And this is because the decay of one thing is the birth of another, in artificial as well as in natural things. For Art follows Nature, and in some parts it improves and surpasses it, just as a weak nature is changed through the Art and diligence of the physician. Nature builds no house, nor does it make an <term>Electuary<note>original: "Electuarium"; a medicinal paste or conserve made by mixing powders with honey or syrup.</note></term> or anything of the kind, since it has no movement of itself to perform such a thing. So also our <term>Stone<note>original: "Stein"; the Philosopher's Stone.</note></term>, although it has the <term>Tincture<note>original: "Tinctur"; the power to color or transform base metals into gold.</note></term> within itself by nature (for it is created perfectly in the earth), nevertheless it has no movement of itself to become a perfect <term>Elixir<note>original: "Elixir"</note></term> unless it is driven and promoted toward it by Art.

Therefore, Art sometimes prepares something which Nature cannot bring to work by itself: but Art follows Nature and completes natural things as far as they are naturally inclined to become perfect; and for that reason, one must come to the aid of Nature through Art in those places where Nature has left off.

<summary>This page discusses the alchemical principle that metals must be reduced to their primary matter to be transformed, arguing that human 'Art' is necessary to complete and perfect the processes started by Nature.</summary>
<keywords>prima materia, transmutation, Art vs Nature, Philosopher's Stone, Elixir, alchemy, Aristotle, medicinal analogy</keywords>

There is also no difference between Nature and Art / except only that Art works from the outside / while Nature works from within. For Art, acting as a tool, provides the motion to Nature / but Nature works of itself / because it strives to reach its own perfection.

Furthermore, every body is either an element / or composed of the elements: But the generation of every composite body consists of the four simple elements. Therefore, it is necessary / that our Stone <note>The "Philosopher's Stone," the goal of the alchemical Great Work.</note> be brought back to the first origin of its Sulfur <term>Sulfur: in alchemy, the active, masculine principle of combustion and soul</term> / and the Mercury <term>Mercury: the passive, feminine principle of fluidity and spirit</term> be divided into the elements / otherwise it can neither be purified / nor put back together / because its most subtle and smallest parts cannot enter into the smallest parts of the body: But when it is divided / then it is purified and composed / and creates an Elixir / which we seek and desire. For the trial of the fire destroys its own particular form / and introduces a new shape.

After the separation of the elements, however, one can see or grasp nothing of them / except only the Earth and the Water. Because one can nowhere see Air and Fire nor perceive their power / except only within the former two elements: Since they are far too thin / subtle and simple / therefore they cannot be beheld at all with physical eyes.

Therefore, you should not [trouble] yourself about them

## CHAPTER II.

There are, however, four elements / and four humors <note>original: "Humores"; the bodily fluids believed to control health and temperament</note> such as blood / choleric moisture <note>yellow bile</note> / phlegmatic moisture <note>phlegm</note> / black bile: And there are four qualities: Moist / warm / cold and dry. The elements are these: Fire / Air / Water / Earth / of which two are inclined toward one another / and two are opposed to one another: Two are active / and two are passive.

Fire and Air can tolerate each other well / as can Water and Earth. Fire and Water are opposed to one another / as are Air and Earth.

Fire and Air are active / Water and Earth are passive: Two rise into the heights / and two betake themselves downward / one is in the middle between the other two / and this is indeed because whenever an opposing thing is far removed from its opposite / they cannot be unified except through the medium <note>the "middle" or mediating element that shares qualities with both opposites</note>.

Heat is opposed to cold / and the moist to the dry: From this it is revealed / that none of these can be united with the other / except through the medium.

Therefore, that which is not opposed / that is

<summary>This page outlines the foundational alchemical theory of the four elements, humors, and qualities, explaining their natural sympathies and antipathies. It emphasizes that opposing forces can only be reconciled through a mediating middle element.</summary>
<keywords>four elements, four humors, alchemy, qualities, active and passive forces, purification, Philosopher's Stone</keywords>

--- Page 119 ---
<meta>Continuity established with the previous page's discussion on the harmony of natural properties; catchword "But" (Doch) connects to the following page.</meta>

united in themselves. Just as the warm part is joined with the dry part—because they are in no way contrary to one another—the element of **Fire** is composed from them.

Likewise, the warm and the moist are not contrary to one another; therefore, the element of **Air** arises from them. The cold and the dry are also not contrary to one another; from them, the element of **Earth** originates. So too, the cold and the moist are not enemies, as it is from them that the element of **Water** gathers itself.

But the warm and the cold cannot be united except through a medium—namely, through the dry and the moist—because otherwise they cannot tolerate being together on their own. This is because one stifles the other due to its contrary property; however, through the moist and the dry, they can indeed exist together, because one preserves the other.

Furthermore, heat and cold bring together and also separate things that are of the same nature (and not dissimilar) through <term>dissolving and coagulating</term><note>original: "dissolviren und coaguliren"; these are the two fundamental actions in alchemy: breaking down a substance into liquid and hardening it back into a solid.</note>. But the moist and the dry are bound and also separated through contraction and moistening.

Therefore, a simple <term>procreation</term><note>original: "Gebährung"; refers here to the "birth" or generation of physical matter.</note> as well as a natural transformation is an effect of the elements. From this, it is manifest that all changeable things are simply generated and naturally transformed through heat and cold.

<summary>This page explains the Aristotelian theory of the four elements, describing how primary qualities like heat, cold, moisture, and dryness combine or conflict to form Fire, Air, Earth, and Water. (108)

Yet, not every thing is produced from every other thing; rather, from a specific thing comes something specific. For there is no true birth except from those things that agree in their nature.

### CHAPTER III.

However, it is called into doubt what that substance might be of which the *Philosophers* <note>original: "Philosophi"; referring here to the ancient and medieval alchemists</note> speak, from which the Philosopher's Stone <term>Stein der Weisen: the legendary alchemical substance capable of turning base metals into gold and granting long life</term> is prepared. This is because none among them names it explicitly; instead, one has proposed this and another has proposed that, even though the truth of it consists in one single thing alone. We truly guard this secret very highly and teach that all other things should be avoided. For it is known from the writings of the Sages that it is only one thing, and nothing foreign shall be added to it; for nothing is suitable for this thing except that which is most closely related to it and of its own nature.

For natural things can bring forth nothing except what is equal to them, and they produce no fruits other than their own. Just as a human begets a human, and a lion begets a lion, so every creature begets its own kind. The mule <note>original: "Maul-Esel"</note>, because it comes from different species <note>original: "Geschlecht"; here meaning lineage or biological category</note>, never conceives nor bears an offspring equal to its parents.

<summary>This page argues that the Philosopher's Stone must be created from a single, specific natural substance, following the biological principle that nature only produces offspring of its own kind.</summary>
<keywords>Philosopher's Stone, alchemy, natural generation, biological species, purity of substance, alchemical theory</keywords>

--- Page 121 ---
(109)

Thus it is with all things that are born of a different nature. Accordingly, when things are of the same kind, their roots are also the same. And so the diversity of things is caused by the difference of their parts. Therefore, learn now to distinguish the pure from the impure, for what a thing does not possess, it cannot give. The Pure is of only one essence without change; the Impure is manifold and composed of opposing parts, and for that reason, it can easily perish. This is evident in Gold and Lead; for **Saturn** <note>original: "Saturnus," the alchemical name for Lead</note>, the lead, can be entirely brought to nothing, but Gold—as the **Sun** <note>original: "Sonne," the alchemical name for Gold</note>—cannot be destroyed at all, and therefore all **Planets** <note>In alchemy, the seven metals were linked to the seven wandering celestial bodies</note> require its light.

Therefore, the knowledge of the opposition of bodies, and the beginning of their creation, makes an easy access to this masterpiece. For one impurity clings to another because they are of the same kind, but the Pure overcomes the nature of the Impure and permits no defect in the mixing. Therefore, you should not seek in Nature what is not within her. Since a thing cannot be procreated except according to its own nature, there is also a greater corruption in the passive thing than in the active one.

The **Tincture** <term>Tincture: a potent alchemical substance capable of transforming or "dyeing" base metals into gold</term> is in the Stone, just as the heart is in a human being. Use, therefore, the noblest and simplest part, and you have enough. The part

<meta>catchword: of the</meta>

<summary>This page discusses the alchemical principle of purity versus impurity, using the stability of Gold (the Sun) and the volatility of Lead (Saturn) to explain how the "Tincture" must be derived from a thing's own nature.</summary>
<keywords>alchemy, purity, Gold, Lead, Saturn, Sun, Tincture, Philosopher's Stone, nature of metals</keywords>

--- Page 122 ---
<meta>The catchword "Es" from the previous page introduces the discussion on the heart and the soul's seat within the body.</meta>

(110)

of the heart from the body is like the part of the brain from the head; for in the heart is the power of the irrational soul, just as in the brain is the power of the rational soul. Our beginning is humble and simple, not mixed, and therefore it is a simple body. So let the mixed things go, and make use of the simple, for it is the first and the last among the planets, like the sun among the stars.

Now I will turn my speech toward those things in nature that are uniform, even though they are very strong. For in those things that possess an equality, a transformation from one into another can easily occur. Therefore, because your question concerns the nature of the two lights of the world <note>The "two lights" typically refer to the Sun (Gold) and the Moon (Silver) in alchemical philosophy.</note>, you must choose therein what is uniform to them. And certainly, because this kind of thing is very properly suited to man, we know that the work cannot be made without him. Hence it is described thus by <term>Aristotle</term> <note>Referring to the "Secretum Secretorum," a popular medieval text falsely attributed to Aristotle, written as advice to Alexander the Great.</note> to <term>Alexander</term>:

> The crowns of kings are adorned with precious stones, by whose beauty the sight is strengthened, the spirit is delighted, and majesty is adorned; and through their power and virtue, highly burdensome diseases are turned away from the bodies, without which all medicine can accomplish little. Therefore, physicians use them in their medicines to drive out burdensome and dangerous diseases.

It is, however, the true proof of the scholars that the best stone among all stones is best suited to drive out all weaknesses. <note>Note here concerning the best stone of all stones, which is to be understood as Gold.</note> For the best stone among all stones is that which is more "cooked" <note>The author uses "cooked" in the alchemical sense of maturation or refinement through heat.</note> and is closest to the fire, which can endure the fire the most, and is more slowly broken by the fire.

Therefore, the noble stones are more useful—namely a ruby and sapphire—than other stones, because they are generated in a warmer place, closer to the sun and in greater heat. And likewise among the metals, gold is more powerful than silver, for it is more cooked; and silver more than copper or any other metal. And just as the ruby contains within itself the effect of all other precious stones, so also gold contains within itself the power of all metals that allow themselves to be expanded <note>malleable metals</note>. For it encompasses all metals within itself, tinctures <term>tincture: in alchemy, the power to transform or imbue a substance with a specific quality or color</term> them, and makes them alive, because it gives its light upwards as far as *Saturn* <note>original: "Saturnum"; in alchemy, Saturn represents Lead</note> and downwards as far as the Moon <note>In alchemy, the Moon represents Silver</note>, because it is nobler than all of them; therefore, you should take gold.

But the ruby has in its temperament <note>original: "Complexion"; referring to the balance of qualities</note> an equality of the elements, because it is for the most part of a bright-shining substance, as if from a clear water; and because it is hardened in a dry and great heat, therefore it cannot be...

forged, nor can it be brought into another state, unless it be entirely destroyed, because it is in every respect contrary to the fire.

Therefore, it can be struck under the hammer, it can be cast, and it is improved in the fire. Because it possesses an equal measure in its good <term>complexion<note>In alchemy, "complexion" refers to the specific balance of the four qualities: hot, cold, moist, and dry.</note></term>, it is more evenly, nobly, and durably composed than all other precious stones.

Just as cold is lacking in Copper and Iron, so heat is lacking in Lead and Tin. And their nature cannot become Gold unless they first become Silver; the reason being that a thing cannot go from the first to the third without passing through the second.

Thus, Gold and Silver are correctly and uniformly composed, and are rightly <term>tempered<note>Balanced or proportioned in their elemental qualities.</note></term> above all other stones that are under heaven, and they belong to the form of our most precious Stone. And in truth, Gold is a lord of stones, the noblest among the bodies, the king and most eminent head among them.

For it is not corrupted by the air, nor by the water, nor by the earth. For every thing increases its own kind, and all these things are uniform in nature, just like the things that grow from the earth; therefore, their increase must also occur in the same manner as the growth of plants.

# CHAPTER IV.

The first material <note>original: "Materia"</note> of the metals is mercury <note>original: "Quecksilber"</note>; for when they are melted, they are transformed back into it. It is certain that every thing consists of that into which it is dissolved. Ice is dissolved into water by means of heat; from this it is then manifest that it was water previously.

Thus all metals can be dissolved into mercury; therefore they were mercury beforehand. You should now take note of this regarding the dissolution of bodies into *mercury* <note>original: "Mercurium"</note>.

So let God be praised, the Creator of excellent natures, who has given us the way and manner to destroy every thing and bring it back again into its first nature and *material*: namely, to bring the pure *prepared* bodies back to the first origin of their *sulfur* <note>original: "Sulphuris"</note> and their *mercury*, so that from them we can make in a few days above the earth that which nature has made under the earth in a thousand years.

Therefore, even if we had nothing more good to expect from *quicksilver* <note>original: "Argento vivo"</note> than that it only makes the bodies subtle, as is its nature, we should nevertheless let that suffice us. For it is dear and pleasant to the metals, and a medium which composes the *tinctures* <note>term: Tinctures; in alchemy, these are the medicinal or transformative essences extracted from substances</note>. For
